nmariano santamaria cuiador de manuscraos
prologo a riano ponce adquirio prominencia en la vida politica filipina desde que fue a espana y colaboro con rizal del pilar lopez jaena y otros en la campana de reformas
fue la suya una labor constante asidua
tenaz eminentemente patriotica
en esa epoca la colonia filipina de madrid contaba con miembros muy ilustres y ponce era uno de ellos
como que ya pasa como un hecho reconocido y aceptado que la direccion de aquel movimiento descansaba principalmente en tres cabezas
rizal del pilar y ponce
rizal era el apostol la encarnacion historica de la patria el alma del nacionalismo naciente y combatiente un espiritu superior en fin lleno de austeridad y respetabilidad
del pilar era el politico militante el periodista luchador el abogado escogido para defender en la brecha diaria un gran pueblo y una gran causa
y ponce mas silencioso mas retirado era el administrador paciente el colaborador util el confidente sin igual en una palabra que se ocupaba de los mil detalles de la campana con su verdadera labor de hormiga
alguien describio el caracter de ponce en estas pocas y certeras palabras
muy sensible ileno de temperamento valiente combatidor constancia extraordinaria receloso pero noble
rizal y del pilar estudiaron y trabajaron lo indecible cada uno en su linea pero sin ponce les hubiera faltado la conexion el complemento la vista a la posteridad
incapaz de traicion con la fe puesta en la patria y en la causa que defendian inteligente estudioso y dotado de una amplia cultura sirvio con absoluta lealtad y honradez
admirador de sus dos companeros tenia afinidad con rizal en su aficion a la historia y al folklore del pais y en su afan por conservar y enaltecer las nativas virtudes y tenia de comuin con del pilar en su temperamento combativo aquella pasion muy bulaqueia que no perdia
oportunidad para acechar al enemigo y asestarle un golpe de bolo forjado con hierro de angat
fue fundador redactor y administrador del periodico la solidaridad secretario de la asociacion hispano filipina de madrid y con del pilar represento en la peninsula a las logias masonicas filipinas cargos todos de absoluta confianza y responsabilidad
fracasado el movimiento de reformas de espana fue a hongkong para unirse a la revolucion
enseguida le hicieron secretario de la junta revolucionaria con agoncillo como presidente
estando los jefes insurrectos en hongkong por el tratado de biak na bato aguinaldo le hizo su secretario privado
cuando se reanudo la revolucion en mayo de a la vuelta de aguinaldo a filipinas el gobierno revolucionario hizo tres nombramientos importantes desde el punto de vista diploma
el de felipe agoncillo como agente plenipotenciario en los estados unidos el de mariano ponce como agente diplomatico en el japon y el de galicano apacible como presidente del comite central de hongkong
fracasada la revolucion por el triunfo de las armas americanas
ponce recogio sus maletas encajono sus documentos y su gran biblioteca y retorno cabizbajo a filipinas
aqui le hicieron sucesivamente director del periodico nacionalista el renacimiento diputado por bulacan a la asamblea filipina e investigador historico de la biblioteca y musco de filipinas
estaba empezando a reanudar sus estudios de historia del pais y de politica contemporanea en el oriente cuando le cogio la muerte
dejo muchos trabajos politicos bibliogrificos e historicos en la prensa tanto de manila como del extranjero dos o tres libros publicados muchos en preparacion y una nutrida correspondencia epistolar en que se revelan las campaiias que hizo en las tres epocas memorables de la historia patria
las cartas que ahora se publican algunas firmadas con pseudonimos como ir
fueron escritas primero cuando era miembro del comite de hongkong y despues cuando descmpinabla cl puesto de agente diplomatico de la revolucion en el japon
son muy interesantes y algunas muy importantes como vera el lector porque descubren hechos internos de la revolucion hasta ahora desconocidos o poco conocidos y revelan intensas actividades en el exterior durante aquellos anos de dura prueba para el pais y sus hombres
antes de partir para el japon ponce mismo preparo en hongkong unas instrucciones para su guia y la de su companero faustino li
prologo vii chauco a las cuales ajusto sus pasos y su conducta con verdadera fidelidad
he aqui dichas instrucciones
instrucciones a que se han de atener los enviados al japon sres
hongkong de junio de
estudiar la politica general del japon respecto al exterior
el grado de relaciones en que se halla con cada una de las principales naciones de europa y america
estudiar su polftica asiatica
sus aspiraciones para lo futuro con respecto al extremo oriente
averiguar que criterio tiene formado el mikado sobre el actual conflicto filipino
inclinarle a nuestro favor bajo el punto de vista de nuestra independencia
averiguar como aprecia la actual intervencion americana en la cuestion filipina
como juzga a los filipinos por la ayuda que estos prestan a los yankees y si el mikado no acierta a adivinar nuestros verdaderos sentimientos y aspiraciones informarle acerca de la verdad de los unos y las otras
bajo esta base inquirir cuanta ayuda puede prestarnos para el logro de estas aspiraciones
impetrar esta ayuda que puede consistir entre otras cosas en emprestitos expediciones secretas de armas negociaciones diplomaticas con las demas naciones en especial la america en el sentido de que estas reconozcan la aptitud del pais para la independencia
proteger esta cuando ilegue el caso por cuantos medios sean necesarios ante la perspectiva de una alianza de estrecha amistad en cuanto se establezca definitivamente la nacionalidad filipina
en el caso de que lleguen las negociaciones al punto de pedir la proteccion del mikado para nuestra independencia averiguar por medios prudentes y en terminos generales las condiciones con que dste nos prestaria su ayuda
estas condiciones solo se podran admitir si estan dentro de los recursos del pais y si no han de perjudicar a este en sus intereses
en caso de duda sobre este particular se consuhtara al gobierno revolucionario a su delegacion en el extranjero antes de entrar en tratos definitivos
en el caso de que america declare sus propositos de anexionarse colonizar a filipinas gestionar la protesta del mikado y por medio de este la de las demas naciones contra
en uiltimno extremo solicitar la intervencion armada del mikado para cuyo caso se ha de procurar que las condiciones que se estipulen esten dentro de la instruccion vi
los comisionados tienen la obligacion de informar y poner al corriente al gobierno a la delegacion de los pasos que se den y del resultado de sus averiguaciones y estudios a fin de recibir de uno o de otra las instrucciones precisas que aconsejen las circunstancias
los comisionados reuniran cuantos datos puedan reco ger pertinentes al objeto de su mision y los resurnirain en una memoria que presentar in al gobierno con el fin de facilitar el estudjo de las cuestiones que se planteen para
su resolucion definitiva
se guardara en todas estas negociaciones la reserva que su indole impone

una manana un sargento cabo se presento a la celda de lauro y le dijo que tenia orden de fusilarle en aquel acto

despues se puso a la disposicion del cabo sargento quien le llevo al patio

la solidaridad se publico durante siete anfos y durante diez anfos hemos proseguido dicha campana

my dear sir
am sending you by parcel post one scarf pin representing our flag
please accept it as a poor souvenir
the blue colour of the sky means our hope in a future prosperity through progress
the red means the blood with which we bought our independence
the white represents the peace which we wish for ours and foreign countries
the sun represents the progress and some times means that the philippine nation belongs to oriental family like japan korea etc
who bear also one sun in their flags
the three stars are the three great groups of islands composing the archipelago viz
the luzon group the bisayas group and the mindanao group
cartas sobre la revolucion
we are working as you know for the recognition of this our flag by foreign countries and hope that japan will be among the first to recognize it

carta a yamagata prejuicios contra la capacidad de los filipinos para el propio gobierno
ocho meses de independencia y sus exitos
no hay manera de que se la entregue a los americanos miyokojf yama kitagata korakigun yokohama february
editor of the yorodzu choho
my dear sir
thank you very much for the kindness with which you received me yesterday
more yet for your honorable appreciation and favorable point of view regarding the filipino question
you are right
we are now fighting against a strong prejudice against our capacity for a self governmey
a few days ago speaking with a journalist who dropp a hint about the question of our incapacity to hold a se government said
is that fanciful affirmation founded on any positive fact
we have very eloquent proofs to the contrary
we have had our government and administration for over eight months and during that time we have organized our provinces and municipalities our finances our army our colleges etc
our parliament has passed a constitution
with what success have we carried on let the whole consular body resident in the philippines answer for me
in a memorandum presented by them to the american government they affirm that under the philippine flag the interests and the persons of foreigners are duly safeguarded
you know where thefts assaults assassination disturbances disorders insecurity of persons and property abuses of authority scandals in the streets
well they are nowhere to be found but in manila governed by the americans
it is not alone that say so but the whole press of the far east
further added that after having shed so much blood and spent so much money in a two years war for their independence the filipinos would in nowise accept the authority of the united states
on the days previous to the rupture between spain and america when we were not sure of our triumph over the spaniards we did not want to sell the said triumph at the price of a promise of annexation to america being at that time in hongkong and belonging to the filipino committee as general secretary can certify that there was in our country a general outcry of protest whenever the question of the possibility that america could substitute spain in the philippines was brought up
how should we give our independence over to the americans now that we have it in our hands
it would be for us an unpardonable crime
next monday th inst
shall have the honor of meeting you in your papers offices at about oclock as we agreed if do not receive from you till that day some letter calling it off
wishing you good health remain yours truly

also co ratulate you for the transformation of the hansei hasshi ine the orient which aims at the promotion of friendly intercourse with a view to mutual assistance between the nations of asia the far east in particular on the basis of common interests a common faith and common customs

lived in europe during ten years but never have been happy till came back here

she desires to share in this community of interests advocated by your magazine
she therefore deserves that the orient defend her cause

for her liberty and independence she fought during two years

affirmation is entirely fanciful

nippon office tokyo
this morning when you allowed me the honor of your visit am very grateful for it forgot to give you some details more about the progressive steps of our government towards the development of our country
in all luzon island we organized postal and telegraphic communications
we put in all principal towns electrical light for public service
all wild tribes living in the interior of mountains as the igorrotes tinguianes itas etc
whom the spaniards during about four centuries of sovereignty could not bring to the civilized life now accept our government and allow us to organize civilly their villages
we have there elements for all branches of science industry art etc the electric plant has been established by filipino engineers
the telegraphic line also by filipino telegraphists
the post and telegraph stamps send yo enclosed have been engraved by filipino lithographists
we have artists known in europe and america as juan luna and felix resurreccion hidalgo painters
de tavera vicente francisco ciriaco gaudinez tanpingko arevalo napkil etc
ulptors all of whom in one time or another have been awarded medals some of them gold medals in european and american exhibitions
luna resurreccion and tavera are members of the society of beaux arts of paris
literary men we have many as shown by the development of our press
within this last eight months moreover new newspapers those enumerated in my interview with a representative of the shanghai daily press namely the el eco de camarines in the camarines province la federacion malaya in batangas province el heraldo de filipinas and el eco de
bisayas in iloilo el eco de ilokos in ilokos province have appeared
about our constitution could not tell you this morning that the former constitution was only for a revolutionary government and of course by decree because then it was impossible to organize a parliamentary body
as soon as we were able to form our parliament we thought of passing a constitution suited to a republican government
this last fundamental law of our republic voted by the parliament was proclaimed on the st of last january
our government wishes independence under the conditions and limitations which we shall stipulate when the americans may reconcile themselves to recognize our independence
during the time intervening between the recognition of our independence and the stipulation of the agreement we can assure public tranquility so that there may not be any reason for foreign intervention in our affairs being to the interest of americans that they protect us against foreign attack in order that we might accomplish our promise
beg you to excuse my bad english
wishing you good health remain dear sir yours fa

my dear sir
am sorry that was not at home when mr
ikutaro aoyagi came here on the day of yesterday and did not therefore get the honour of saluting him
feel very deep gratitude for your kind letter and your benevolence of inviting me to the meeting of the shoku min kyokwai important society devoted to the study of colonial problems and presided by viscount buyo enemoto to take place on the th inst
should attend at it very willingly because it would be a good occasion for explaining once more before the japanese people my grateful sentiments for this sympathy towards our cause
but my health unfortunately does not allow me to take advantage of such a good opportunity
by my physicians prescription am advised to go to hakone and take there some sulphur bath on next thursday
for this reason it is impossible for me to accept your kind invitation in this time but hope that in near future shall be able to accomplish my earnest desire of saluting personally to the shoku minkyokwai and its members
meanwhile beg you present my deep respect to this society and expecting that you may excuse me and also to see you on a near time remain dear sir yours ever sincerely mariano ponce

my dear sir
want all of your kindness in order to pardon me for my long negligence to answer your kind letter of the th inst
and also to thank you for your interesting and patriotic book nippon shugi kikkyo ron sent to me and for your sympathy toward us
have not words to express you my hearty feelings
please add to the coldness of utterances expressed in english the hotness of my cordial sentiments
during the last week was and now am yet under the medical prescription and for this consideration hope that you may excuse me
feel also very deep gratitude for your generous interest towards the success of our republic
as you know all those rumors of surrendering of philippine army were quite unground
small number of inhabitants of manila are accepting the american sovereignty but nobody of them belongs to the revolutionary party
most of them joined to us only when they looked our triumphs against the spaniards
they are the cold spirited people who exist always everywhere
their adhesion of course to the americans has no importance for the cause of
cartas sobre la revolucion
because all those sincere revolutionaries who have been always from the beginning on the work and poured out money and blood all remain strongly against the ambitious strangers
no fear that they may also surrender
they are ready to undergo every events as they also know your maxim and motto perseverance conquers all things

xlii with one book in spanish on the christianism in the ancient tagalog civilization

fujita will be good enough to explain to you its matters

con fecha de julio nuestro gobierno envio una circular a
los consules extranjeros residentes en las islas exponiendoles las razones que tiene para retener a los prisioneros espanoles que son mas de en la actualidad
el principal argumento es que nuestro gobierno espera que el gobierno de espana gestione oficialmente la libertad de dichos prisioneros lo que espana no quiere hacer porque esto implicaria reconocimiento de nuestra independencia lo cual le prohibe el tratado de paris
este tratado es nulo porque cuando se firmo ya no tenia poder ni soberania espana sobre las islas
pues meses antes los defensores y representantes de dicha soberania ya habian rendido sus armas a nuestras tropas y todos fueron hechos prisioneros
espana pues no puede ceder a los estados unidos una soberania que ya no posela y menos los estados unidos pueden aceptar esa cesion de una cosa que no existe
como la prueba patente de que esa soberania espanola ya no existe el de diciembre ultimo en que se firmo dicho tratado de paris nuestro gobierno declaro y asi lo hizo saber a los consules que mientras espaia los estados unidos no reconozcan esa independencia nuestro gobierno se vera en la dolorosa necesidad de retener en su poder los prisioneros espanoles

absence from japan and serious indisposition necessitating a trip to sunnier climates have prevented me from receiving and replying to your most recent letters up to this time
thank you most heartily for your interesting letters and for the kind offer to visit the states in behalf of my unhappy and cruelly oppressed countrymen with a view to influencing votes for the anti expansion party in the november states elections
it is unfortunately too late now to give effect to your thoughtful suggestion and all we can do is correct as speedily as possible the most damaging among the false and grossly misleading reports which are so often published in american papers and from them copied by your canadian and other journals this is while we are expecting reply from our government as already informed you in my last letter
with this object in view as a preliminary step we shall be glad to avail ourselves of your good service to receive from us telegrams containing the filipino version of occurrences in the philippines and at times contradicting false malicious and damaging yankee canards
cartas sobre la revolucion
in order to effect this purpose it is necessary
a that you lose no time in placing us in telegraphic communication with you the cable address being made as brief as possible to save expense
that as soon as you can arrange with some british cable company to wire instruction to the manager in hongkong of the eastern extension australasia and china telegraph company to accept messages prepaid at press rates signed chesney and addressed as adviced by you
we shall pay cash in advance here on each message sent to you
that as soon as you receive our news messages you get them printed and published in the gazette or other prominent journals in your city and promptly mark the news and post copies of the paper containing it to a few of the leading papers in each state of the united states of america
that whenever possible you cause some article or leader rette to be published in the gazette calling attention to the news wired by us
e that you cause the news to be published as coming from the papers far eastern of hongkong correspondent
this is especially desirable because if you publish it as coming from the hongkong filipino committee or junta it will carry less weight and the paper publishing it will be regarded although wrongfully as a paid special agent of the filipinos and consequently its utterances would in all probability lose such force in america
it might be announced however that the pa
pers special correspondent is able to get information direct from the filipino government which accounts for it being somewhat tardy at times the censorship in manila necessitating the adoption of a circuitous route
it is very desirable that copies of each issue containing our news be forwarded without fail to

my reason for wishing to get our reports into print promptly is based on our desire to give the american people an opportunity to form proper judgment of the issues envolved in mckinleys aggressive campaign in our islands and enable them also to place a true value on the doctored news which the american press censors and semi official newsmonger make to be published in american dailies
it is impossible to establish direct relations with any american paper because our messages would be liable to confiscation delay and mutilation as soon as they get on american wire
by wiring to canada our news will trend entirely on british wire and if promptly published and posted over the frontier it will serve our purpose and no doubt prove quite satisfactory
if you will kindly undertake to arrange for receipt of our message at the earliest moment possible at press rate and will be good enough to carry out our wishes as expressed in this letter you are welcome to now draw on me through one of your banks for
two hundred dollars to cover expenses until can get your reply to this and learn from you what expenses you incur or are likely to incur in our behalf
may ask that you take action as soon as you get this letter in order that should we desire it we can commence telegraphing to you about the middle of december
shall be glad to receive from you two copies of each issue of the paper which published our news telegrams also if you can find time to do it clippings from canadian and other american papers that comments on the filipino news
the question of appointing you our especial representative for the whole of world american continent is now under con

sideration by our government as have foresaid to you and as soon as receive their instruction will promptly communicate with
in the meantime allow me to tender our most hearty good wishes for the success of all your undertakings
we hope you will expend a very happy christmas and the new year now fast approaching will be happy and prosperous one for you and all who are near and dear to you
remain dear sir yours very truly
